# 11512 — lemma inventory and Clementine pre-scan

**Commentaria in Canticum canticorum** · PL 195 · cols 1001–1278A · 81 chunks.

Generated at launch. **This file is a CANDIDATE LIST, not a findings list.**

Every marked span in the Latin of this work, in order, with its column band — **both the
italic `*…*` spans and the quoted `« … »` spans.** ⛔ Which of the two carries the verse
lemma VARIES BY BOOK and may not be assumed: Luke (9000) has no guillemets at all and sets
its lemmata in italic, while Genesis (8950) sets 1,207 lemmata in guillemets and italicizes
only second lemmata, Hebrew words and the *Historice/Allegorice* tags. Spans opening a
`VERS. n.--` address are the verse lemmata proper; the rest are second lemmata picked up
mid-gloss, and inline scripture the gloss quotes. **7a″ applies to all of them.**

## How the ✓ / ⚠ marks were made, and what they do NOT mean

Mechanical phrase search of the normalized span against
`sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt` (all 73 books, one verse per line).

- **✓ followed by an ADDRESS** = this exact word sequence occurs, as whole words, in the
  verse(s) named. ⛔ Still **not clearance** — but now you can see WHERE it matched, which is
  the thing a bare tick used to hide. **Read the address.** If you are glossing Luke 2 and the
  mark says `✓ Lc 19:38`, the mark has just told you the lemma matches a different verse.
- **⚑ MATCHES ONLY ELSEWHERE** = the span carries a `VERS.` address, so it is the verse lemma
  proper, and it occurs in the Clementine — but **not in this book and chapter.** In a gospel
  commentary that is usually the harmony answering for the wrong evangelist. Measured on 9000:
  six spans scored a bare ✓ and every one was a real divergence found by a stint walking the
  line — `Quid cogitatis mala…` off Mt 9:4 inside a commentary on Lc 5:22, and `Gloria in
  excelsis Deo` at Lc 2:14 off Lc 19:38, the verse the gloss expounds two lines later.
- **⚠ NOT in Clementine verbatim** = the sequence was not found. Most of these will be
  innocent: a fragment cut mid-phrase, an `usque ad` abridgment span, a span carrying an
  editorial `etc.`, a Migne spelling. **A ⚠ is a place to look, never a divergence.**
- Single words are not searchable this way and are marked for checking in place.

## ⚠ THE COMPARAND ITSELF HAS DEFECTS — CHECK BEFORE YOU ACCUSE MIGNE

`clementine-flat.txt` is Tweedale's 2005 "Quasimodo" release and **the upstream
correction diffs published after it are deliberately NOT applied** (see
`sources/vulgate/README.md`). So a divergence can live on OUR side of the collation, and
a `[var:]` fired against it is a false public claim about Migne's text.

**Confirmed defects in this copy** (found by stints, both forms of the file affected):

    Is 22:18   `Coronas cornonabit te`   — read `Coronans coronabit te`

⛔ **The test is cheap and it is yours to run: if the brief's Clementine reading is not
itself sound Latin, the defect is in the comparand.** `cornonabit` is not a word. Say so,
collate against the sense, and do NOT fire a marker on it. Add any new instance to this
list. ⚑ The same rule applies at one remove to a form that IS sound but looks wrong: a
peer declined a `[sic:]` on Migne's `requiscere` because this file prints the syncopated
stem at the verse quoted — the form lives outside Migne's compositor.

⛔ **You still walk every line.** The marks order your work; they do not do it. A `[var:]`
is a public claim about another text and must rest on the file, cited by book chapter:verse —
never on this pre-scan and never on recollection.

[1006A] *Commentaria venerabilis domini abbatis Wolberonis, licet devotis ac Deo consecratis virginibus quondam ita postulantibus, ab auctore ipso conscripta et inscripta fuerint, volueritque cum sanctimonialibus solis illa delitescere, neque communem in lucem prodire nisi, ab aliquo correcta in opus elaboratum ac masculinum evaserint: nolim tibi persuasum habeas, lector benevole, quasi opera mea hoc ipsum assecutus fueris. Haec namque vere de se humiliter sentientium virtus est magna et praeclara agere aut scribere et nihilominus parve et mediocriter de se, suisque sentire. Emicat in hac virtutis specie non vulgariter abbas noster Wolbero. in cujus commentariis licet antiquissimis, sed assiduo annorum novem studio congestis tantum abest ut informe aliquid, vel muliebre aut lima emendatiori indigens offenderim (licet in dedicatione religiosis sororibus suis nuncupata, aliisque operis, hujus praefationibus hoc ipsum innuere videatur) ut divino magis quam humano spiritu conceptum et calamo exaratum opusculum hoc videri merito debeat. Quapropter latere te nolim quicunque operis hujus lector futurus es parum me Commentariis hisce de proprio aut addidisse aut miscuisse, nisi ubi sensus ambiguitas, periodorum dissolutio versuumque disjunctio simile quid desiderare visum fuerat. Haec fortassis erant, quae lectorem delicatum in Wolberone antiquo offendere potuissent. Quibus cum labore et conatu meo sat subventum, nescio qua typographi vel correctoris incuria aut festinatione errata nonnulla hinc inde irrepserint, quae sub finem libri hujus emendata, videre liceat, operam hanc meam qualemcunque aequi bonique consulito, et dum ea frueris in tui emolumentum suscipe, et suscipe benigne perpetuum auctoris hujus monumentum. Vale*   ⚠ NOT in Clementine verbatim — CHECK
